pew_work_family_detailed prompts

Review every consequential prompt before model execution.

Support point 1: declared response-pattern anchor
Support point identifier: pew_work_family_detailed_001

Survey context: A synthetic augmentation of Pew Research Center American Trends Panel Wave 154 for U.S. adults. The five gender-comparison marginals are observed September 2024 estimates. All other candidate moments are explicitly model-synthetic 2026 estimates and retain separate provenance.
Battery topic: gender attitudes, family circumstances, marriage, children, paid work, household labor, and financial bargaining among U.S. adults

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- hobbies: Men and women are basically similar
- physical_abilities: Men and women are basically different
- parenting: Men and women are basically similar
- feelings: Men and women are basically different
- workplace: Men and women are basically similar
- relationship_status: Not currently in a relationship
- children_at_home: No children under 18
- household_income: Less than $50,000
- marriage_importance: Not at all important
- parenthood_importance: Not at all important
- paid_work_priority: Somewhat important
- childcare_division: Both parents should divide it about equally
- housework_division: Both adults should divide it about equally
- financial_decision_style: The person who manages the money should usually decide
- career_sacrifice_rule: It depends entirely on the family

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a readable second-person synthesis in "persona", beginning with "Your " or "You ". Do not compress distinct
answers into labels such as "traditional", "egalitarian", or "mixed". In "persona_details", write one explicit,
second-person sentence for every item, using the exact item keys shown in the schema. Each sentence must state the
substantive position or circumstance represented by that item's declared response pattern. The complete persona will be
assembled from the synthesis and every detail, so its length should grow with the battery. Include only persona
description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities.

Then provide subjective response probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order,
contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to 1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- hobbies: Their hobbies and personal interests
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- physical_abilities: Their physical abilities
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- parenting: Their approach to parenting
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- feelings: How they express their feelings
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- workplace: The things they are good at in the workplace
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- relationship_status: Current relationship situation
  Options, in output order: 1. Married; 2. Living with a partner; 3. In a relationship but not living together; 4. Not currently in a relationship
- children_at_home: Children under 18 in the household
  Options, in output order: 1. No children under 18; 2. At least one child under 6; 3. Only children ages 6 to 17; 4. Children both under 6 and ages 6 to 17
- household_income: Annual household income
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than $50,000; 2. $50,000 to $99,999; 3. $100,000 to $149,999; 4. $150,000 or more
- marriage_importance: Personal importance of marriage
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- parenthood_importance: Personal importance of raising children
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- paid_work_priority: Personal importance of paid-work or career success
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- childcare_division: Preferred division of day-to-day childcare
  Options, in output order: 1. One parent should do most of it; 2. Both parents should divide it about equally; 3. The parent with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the family
- housework_division: Preferred division of routine housework
  Options, in output order: 1. One person should do most of it; 2. Both adults should divide it about equally; 3. The person with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the household
- financial_decision_style: Preferred style for major household financial decisions
  Options, in output order: 1. The higher earner should usually decide; 2. The person who manages the money should usually decide; 3. Both adults should decide together; 4. It depends on the decision
- career_sacrifice_rule: Preferred rule for deciding who reduces paid work
  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family

Return only valid JSON with exactly this schema:
{
  "persona": "You are ...",
  "persona_details": {
    "hobbies": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "physical_abilities": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "parenting": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "feelings": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "workplace": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "relationship_status": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "children_at_home": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "household_income": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "marriage_importance": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "parenthood_importance": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "paid_work_priority": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "childcare_division": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "housework_division": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "financial_decision_style": "You explicitly believe or experience ...",
    "career_sacrifice_rule": "You explicitly believe or experience ..."
  },
  "probabilities": {
    "hobbies": [numbers in option order],
    "physical_abilities": [numbers in option order],
    "parenting": [numbers in option order],
    "feelings": [numbers in option order],
    "workplace": [numbers in option order],
    "relationship_status": [numbers in option order],
    "children_at_home": [numbers in option order],
    "household_income": [numbers in option order],

- career_sacrifice_rule: Preferred rule for deciding who reduces paid work
  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family

- career_sacrifice_rule: Preferred rule for deciding who reduces paid work
  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family

  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family

Survey context: A synthetic augmentation of Pew Research Center American Trends Panel Wave 154 for U.S. adults. The five gender-comparison marginals are observed September 2024 estimates. All other candidate moments are explicitly model-synthetic 2026 estimates and retain separate provenance.
Battery topic: gender attitudes, family circumstances, marriage, children, paid work, household labor, and financial bargaining among U.S. adults

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- hobbies: Men and women are basically similar
- physical_abilities: Men and women are basically different
- parenting: Men and women are basically different
- feelings: Men and women are basically similar
- workplace: Men and women are basically similar
- relationship_status: Not currently in a relationship
- children_at_home: Only children ages 6 to 17
- household_income: $50,000 to $99,999
- marriage_importance: Not too important
- parenthood_importance: Very important
- paid_work_priority: Somewhat important
- childcare_division: The parent with more available time should do more
- housework_division: One person should do most of it
- financial_decision_style: The higher earner should usually decide
- career_sacrifice_rule: Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a readable second-person synthesis in "persona", beginning with "Your " or "You ". Do not compress distinct
answers into labels such as "traditional", "egalitarian", or "mixed". In "persona_details", write one explicit,
second-person sentence for every item, using the exact item keys shown in the schema. Each sentence must state the
substantive position or circumstance represented by that item's declared response pattern. The complete persona will be
assembled from the synthesis and every detail, so its length should grow with the battery. Include only persona
description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities.

Then provide subjective response probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order,
contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to 1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- hobbies: Their hobbies and personal interests
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- physical_abilities: Their physical abilities
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- parenting: Their approach to parenting
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- feelings: How they express their feelings
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- workplace: The things they are good at in the workplace
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- relationship_status: Current relationship situation
  Options, in output order: 1. Married; 2. Living with a partner; 3. In a relationship but not living together; 4. Not currently in a relationship
- children_at_home: Children under 18 in the household
  Options, in output order: 1. No children under 18; 2. At least one child under 6; 3. Only children ages 6 to 17; 4. Children both under 6 and ages 6 to 17
- household_income: Annual household income
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than $50,000; 2. $50,000 to $99,999; 3. $100,000 to $149,999; 4. $150,000 or more
- marriage_importance: Personal importance of marriage
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- parenthood_importance: Personal importance of raising children
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- paid_work_priority: Personal importance of paid-work or career success
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- childcare_division: Preferred division of day-to-day childcare
  Options, in output order: 1. One parent should do most of it; 2. Both parents should divide it about equally; 3. The parent with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the family
- housework_division: Preferred division of routine housework
  Options, in output order: 1. One person should do most of it; 2. Both adults should divide it about equally; 3. The person with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the household
- financial_decision_style: Preferred style for major household financial decisions
  Options, in output order: 1. The higher earner should usually decide; 2. The person who manages the money should usually decide; 3. Both adults should decide together; 4. It depends on the decision
- career_sacrifice_rule: Preferred rule for deciding who reduces paid work
  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family

Survey context: A synthetic augmentation of Pew Research Center American Trends Panel Wave 154 for U.S. adults. The five gender-comparison marginals are observed September 2024 estimates. All other candidate moments are explicitly model-synthetic 2026 estimates and retain separate provenance.
Battery topic: gender attitudes, family circumstances, marriage, children, paid work, household labor, and financial bargaining among U.S. adults

Construct a synthetic survey-response profile that preserves every cell in this declared answer blueprint. Unusual combinations are intentional; do not replace them with a more stereotypically coherent profile.
- hobbies: Men and women are basically similar
- physical_abilities: Men and women are basically different
- parenting: Men and women are basically similar
- feelings: Men and women are basically different
- workplace: Men and women are basically different
- relationship_status: Living with a partner
- children_at_home: No children under 18
- household_income: $50,000 to $99,999
- marriage_importance: Very important
- parenthood_importance: Somewhat important
- paid_work_priority: Not at all important
- childcare_division: Both parents should divide it about equally
- housework_division: The person with more available time should do more
- financial_decision_style: It depends on the decision
- career_sacrifice_rule: It depends entirely on the family

Follow the complete response pattern explicitly supplied below.
For every declared blueprint cell, assign that option the largest probability in its item vector. The persona must make the complete combination understandable without changing any declared answer.
Do not invent demographic characteristics unless the design explicitly supplies them.

Write a readable second-person synthesis in "persona", beginning with "Your " or "You ". Do not compress distinct
answers into labels such as "traditional", "egalitarian", or "mixed". In "persona_details", write one explicit,
second-person sentence for every item, using the exact item keys shown in the schema. Each sentence must state the
substantive position or circumstance represented by that item's declared response pattern. The complete persona will be
assembled from the synthesis and every detail, so its length should grow with the battery. Include only persona
description—not instructions about answering questions or probabilities.

Then provide subjective response probabilities for every item. Each vector must follow the displayed option order,
contain nonnegative numbers, and sum to 1. Each probability must be at least 0.01.

Items and response options:
- hobbies: Their hobbies and personal interests
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- physical_abilities: Their physical abilities
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- parenting: Their approach to parenting
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- feelings: How they express their feelings
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- workplace: The things they are good at in the workplace
  Options, in output order: 1. Men and women are basically similar; 2. Men and women are basically different
- relationship_status: Current relationship situation
  Options, in output order: 1. Married; 2. Living with a partner; 3. In a relationship but not living together; 4. Not currently in a relationship
- children_at_home: Children under 18 in the household
  Options, in output order: 1. No children under 18; 2. At least one child under 6; 3. Only children ages 6 to 17; 4. Children both under 6 and ages 6 to 17
- household_income: Annual household income
  Options, in output order: 1. Less than $50,000; 2. $50,000 to $99,999; 3. $100,000 to $149,999; 4. $150,000 or more
- marriage_importance: Personal importance of marriage
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- parenthood_importance: Personal importance of raising children
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- paid_work_priority: Personal importance of paid-work or career success
  Options, in output order: 1. Not at all important; 2. Not too important; 3. Somewhat important; 4. Very important
- childcare_division: Preferred division of day-to-day childcare
  Options, in output order: 1. One parent should do most of it; 2. Both parents should divide it about equally; 3. The parent with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the family
- housework_division: Preferred division of routine housework
  Options, in output order: 1. One person should do most of it; 2. Both adults should divide it about equally; 3. The person with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the household
- financial_decision_style: Preferred style for major household financial decisions
  Options, in output order: 1. The higher earner should usually decide; 2. The person who manages the money should usually decide; 3. Both adults should decide together; 4. It depends on the decision
- career_sacrifice_rule: Preferred rule for deciding who reduces paid work
  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family

  Options, in output order: 1. One person should do most of it; 2. Both adults should divide it about equally; 3. The person with more available time should do more; 4. It depends entirely on the household

- career_sacrifice_rule: Preferred rule for deciding who reduces paid work
  Options, in output order: 1. The lower earner should reduce work; 2. The adult with the more flexible job should reduce work; 3. Both adults should reduce work as equally as possible; 4. It depends entirely on the family
